# Seshat BI -- Idea Bank

> **This is a future-idea bank, not a roadmap and not a commitment.** Nothing here is
> planned, scheduled, or approved. It is exploratory brainstorming output to browse for
> inspiration. The authoritative roadmap is `docs/roadmap/roadmap.md` (F-numbered features).

_Re-running regenerates this file as a HISTORY-AWARE snapshot: each run still re-reasons every
_idea from scratch, but it now READS the prior bank and Ground's verified ship-status first, so
_an idea that has since SHIPPED is recorded as shipped (with its evidence) and a settled rejection
_is not re-litigated. This is still an idea BANK, not an evolving plan: nothing here is promoted,
_scheduled, or committed. A SHIPPED tag only records that a human already took an equivalent idea
_through the normal feature process -- the engine never moves an idea onto the roadmap itself. The
_verdicts and scores are an automated reviewer's triage opinion. An idea advances only through the
_normal spec/feature process, with a human decision._

_Generated by the `idea-engine` workflow. Generated on 2026-08-22._

> **DEGRADED RUN: adversarial skeptic did not challenge 1 candidate(s) -- those were clamped (killed, demoted from ADOPT) for unproven coverage. Treat this bank as partial.**

**46 ideas scored** (generated in 1 round; raw pre-dedupe 47). Verdicts: ADOPT 8, CONSIDER 9, PARK 20, REJECT 8, SHIPPED 1. Horizon: NOW 31, HORIZON 15.

## Reviewer portfolio verdict

> 43 candidates reviewed across four panelist lenses (principle auditor, duplication auditor, value/feasibility realist, design-foundation reviewer). Six clear ADOPTs: c10 (Domain Pack Interview Cards), c19 (widen dax_gen past base|ratio), c22 (fix-at-point-of-failure annotations), c27 (token ref-pointer resolution guard), c29 (section-vocabulary parity), c35 (answer freshness header), and c41 (fix ONBOARDING.md) -- these had near-unanimous ADOPT/CONSIDER support and verified, concrete deliverables. c2 (EXPLAIN-plan review surface) also lands as ADOPT on strong scores despite one reviewer holding at CONSIDER. A wide CONSIDER band (c1, c12, c18, c21, c23, c28, c33, c34, c43, c45) is worth a second look -- mostly solid ideas held back by one dissenting reviewer's mechanism or feasibility concern. The PARK pile (about half the set) is dominated by ideas gated on absent substrate: only retail_store_sales has the metrics/design/handoff artifacts needed, so anything requiring cross-table folding or owner-authored fields (c4, c11, c13, c17, c30-c32, c36, c38, c40, c42, c44-c46) stalls until readiness advances. REJECTs cluster around two failure modes: premises falsified by disk inspection (c8, c15, c25, c26, c37, c39) and candidates that shipped-duplication auditors found already covered on disk (c3 "RC Default Catalog" and c6 "Contract Meaning Transfer" both flagged SHIPPED by at least one reviewer, c16 similarly). Five candidates carry an eligibility split where one reviewer ruled the idea structurally ineligible while others scored it substantively (c11, c20, c25, c28) -- these need a ruling on the eligibility question itself before value/feasibility scores mean anything.

## Legend

- **Verdict** (reviewer's *triage opinion only* -- not a decision to build) -- ADOPT (worth a closer look first) - CONSIDER (interesting; needs a decision or dependency) - PARK (horizon / later) - REJECT (ineligible or conflicts -- kept for the record).
- **How a verdict is reached** (deterministic, computed in JS -- no model picks the final label): the panel verdict is the one 2 or more of the 4 reviewers chose; with no such majority the MORE CAUTIOUS verdict present wins. A demote-only clamp then applies -- if the reviewers split on eligibility (or the panel was short a reviewer) ADOPT is capped at CONSIDER, if all four ruled it ineligible it becomes REJECT, and an idea the adversarial skeptic never challenged is capped out of ADOPT for unproven coverage. Nothing is ever promoted upward.
- **Why ADOPT is rare**: it requires 2+ of 4 reviewers to independently choose ADOPT *and* a unanimous full-panel eligibility pass. Reviewers are told to default to caution and the skeptic defaults to refuted, so most genuinely good ideas land on CONSIDER. A run with 0 ADOPT is the normal outcome, not a failure -- read CONSIDER as the top recommendation and compare ideas by their V/F scores.
- **Horizon** -- `NOW` (fits the repo today) - `HORIZON` (future vision).
- **Eligibility** -- respects all hard principles, or violates one (named in the rationale).
- **V / F** -- value / feasibility (1-10), reviewer-assigned.
- **SHIPPED / SETTLED** -- a prior idea Memory matched to shipped work or a settled rejection; kept for the record, not an open candidate.

## Design Foundation

_A first-class CATEGORICAL cohort of the Power BI presentation FOUNDATION layer
(`strengthens_layer = design-system`): theme-token contracts, background/canvas
conventions, layout blueprints, accessibility/contrast, design-review evidence --
governance of the design layer, never report authoring. This is a cross-reference
grouping, not a re-score: each idea keeps its existing verdict and is detailed in
its verdict section above. No numeric score is attached here (roadmap hard rule #9),
and the lane never promotes an idea onto the roadmap or assigns an F-row (Principle V)._

- **Bilingual Report Round Trip -- term dictionary to report vocabulary** -- PARK - `HORIZON`
- **Theme-Pair Declaration Coverage Report (which design artifacts opted into which floor)** -- REJECT - `NOW`
- **Token Ref-Pointer Resolution Guard (dangling `*_ref` and `grid_ref` paths)** -- ADOPT - `NOW`
- **Spacing and Type-Scale Rhythm Closure Check** -- CONSIDER - `NOW`
- **Section-Vocabulary Parity Across Grid, Mobile Grid and Blueprints** -- ADOPT - `NOW`
- **Mobile Reflow-Fate Contract (a blueprint may not claim a desktop_only band survives)** -- PARK - `HORIZON`
- **Peer-Theme Parity Contract (a second theme must be a structural peer of the first)** -- PARK - `HORIZON`
- **Preview-Gallery Token Drift Detector (the 15 HTML previews vs the YAML tokens)** -- PARK - `HORIZON`
- **Design-Review Evidence Provenance Chain (which artifact versions the reviewer actually saw)** -- CONSIDER - `NOW`
- **Caveat and Provenance Placement Convention** -- PARK - `HORIZON`

**Why this verdict:** [HARD-PRINCIPLE AUDITOR. I presume every idea INELIGIBLE until it proves it touches only reasoning, static-text, or read-only seams. Duplication, absent substrate, thin residue and unenforced conventions are NOT principle violations -- I record those as low value with an empty ineligibility_reason so the eligibility column keeps discriminating between forbidden and merely worthless. I verified the load-bearing claims on disk myself: docs/decisions/0002-retail-cleaning-defaults.md defines RC1-RC16 with rationale; comparison_baseline.py (CB1) already requires a primary date field; snapshot_time_additivity.py (HR5) already ERRORs on absent/`fully` time_additivity; dax_gen.py:262 refuses any kind outside base|ratio; rule_fix_table.py has only a script and its test as consumers; executive-dark-design-tokens.yaml DOES declare min_text_contrast_ratio (c26's premise is false); tower-retail-design-tokens.yaml declares sizes and base_unit_px but NO ordering contract. The supplied SHIP STATUS is partial -- CB1, HR5, HR13, HR4, H3 and the DL/CT design families are live in src/seshat/rules/ and absent from it -- so any novelty argument resting on that list alone is unsound.] Cleanest eligibility profile in the set. I reward governance/linting/evidence seams that make the design layer CHECKABLE and discount cosmetic ideas with no durable seam.

I read the design corpus on disk before scoring rather than trusting the supplied history: design/tokens/ (two files: tower-retail, executive-dark), design/grids/ (16x9-grid.yaml, mobile-grid.yaml), themes/ (two theme JSONs + executive-dark.theme-spec.md), reports/blueprints/ (four filled blueprints), and the ten live design rules in src/seshat/rules/ (design_contrast CT1, design_ramp_deltae, design_categorical_distinctness, design_grid_closure DL5, design_review_evidence DL4, design_visual_selfcheck DL6, design_theme_fidelity DL3, design_theme, design_background, design_routes).

Three verified facts drove my design verdicts. (1) BOTH token files declare accessibility.min_text_contrast_ratio, and NEITHER declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae -- so c26's premise ("a dark dashboard can ship entirely un-contrast-checked") is false, and its proposed ERROR is structurally unfireable. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] Outside my design standpoint; low confidence, deferring to the SQL/architect lens. Noting only that the shape is sound by my criteria: a zero-consumer corpus activated, human-supplied input so no DB and no driver gate, and categorical findings terminating on a committed checklist rather than an invented score.

**Panel dissent:** Three reviewers ADOPT (v7/f7); the value/feasibility realist held at CONSIDER and the design reviewer at CONSIDER (v6/f7), both citing the same v7/f7 numbers as the ADOPT camp but reading the bar differently.

**First step:** Fix the input contract for a pasted plan (what a minimal acceptable EXPLAIN ANALYZE dump must contain) and confirm the plan-review checklist is the terminating artifact.

### Widen dax_gen beyond base|ratio: the variance/two-table contract shape

`NOW` - **V8 / F6** - consistency: consistent - respects principles

**Why this verdict:** [HARD-PRINCIPLE AUDITOR. I presume every idea INELIGIBLE until it proves it touches only reasoning, static-text, or read-only seams. I name a violated principle ONLY for the five named classes: executes (never_execute), bypasses a gate, fabricates a confidence score, self-grants approval (Principle V / never_self_grant_approval), or is C086-specific. Duplication, absent substrate, thin residue and unenforced conventions are NOT principle violations -- I record those as low value with an empty ineligibility_reason so the eligibility column keeps discriminating between forbidden and merely worthless. I verified the load-bearing claims on disk myself: docs/decisions/0002-retail-cleaning-defaults.md defines RC1-RC16 with rationale; comparison_baseline.py (CB1) already requires a primary date field; snapshot_time_additivity.py (HR5) already ERRORs on absent/`fully` time_additivity; dax_gen.py:262 refuses any kind outside base|ratio; rule_fix_table.py has only a script and its test as consumers; executive-dark-design-tokens.yaml DOES declare min_text_contrast_ratio (c26's premise is false); tower-retail-design-tokens.yaml declares sizes and base_unit_px but NO ordering contract. The supplied SHIP STATUS is partial -- CB1, HR5, HR13, HR4, H3 and the DL/CT design families are live in src/seshat/rules/ and absent from it -- so any novelty argument resting on that list alone is unsound.] I pressed hardest here because generating DAX is the closest thing in this set to authoring semantics, and it holds. Generating text is not executing it -- nothing connects, nothing deploys, and the acceptance oracle is the SHIPPED metric_drift.check_measure_drift, so anything that does not round-trip is a refusal rather than a best-effort measure: fail-closed, not fail-open. The two-gold-table binding tension is routed to an owner ruling, not to the emitter, which is the seam that keeps Principle V intact. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] MY LANE and my strongest ADOPT; my own evidence strengthens it past the skeptic's 'survived'. I verified the pointer graph is genuinely unresolved by any RULE: `grep -rln` for value_typography_ref/label_typography_ref/background_ref/grid_ref across src/ returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py. Five dotted pointers live in tower-retail-design-tokens.yaml (sentiment_color_ref -> colors.sentiment, value_typography_ref -> typography.scale_pt.kpi_value, label_typography_ref -> typography.scale_pt.kpi_label, background_ref -> colors.surface, grid_ref -> design/grids/16x9-grid.yaml) plus grid_ref and tokens_ref in the filled blueprints. Rename a token key or move a grid file and the KPI-card rules plus four blueprints break in total silence. This is the ideal design-layer guard by my criteria: a dangling pointer is OBJECTIVELY wrong, so the rule decides nothing about design quality (Principle V is untouched), it is pure dotted-path lookup plus a tracked-file existence test, and it makes the design layer checkable rather than prettier. Distinct from DL3, whose docstring I read -- DL3 reconciles token-to-theme VALUES under declared correspondence; this resolves intra-file and cross-file REFERENCES. The suffix-discovery weakness is a spec detail, not a defect in the guard.

**Panel dissent:** Two reviewers ADOPT (v6-7/f8); the value/feasibility realist holds at CONSIDER (v6/f7) wanting more proof the fix generalizes past the one verified dead-pointer instance.

**First step:** Enumerate every pointer-shaped key in the tokens and blueprint corpora by hand first, so the suffix convention is validated against the real key set rather than assumed.

Note the skeptic array omits c23 entirely, so I scored it on my own read.] I read the file: it is verbatim a Claude Code usage-stats template -- bar charts of Ahmed's /usage, /model and /effort call counts and a CodeScene MCP tally -- with nothing about the seven stages, the check/validate split, or the approval seams. The most obviously-named front door answers a different question entirely, which is the purest adoption defect available and the cheapest to fix, since the content exists across README, COMPASS and readiness-model and only the routing order is missing. One correction I carry into the rationale: the candidate claims moving the usage material elsewhere keeps the ShareOnboardingGuide flow working, but that tool uploads ONBOARDING.md BY NAME, so rewriting it changes what teammates receive. That is arguably the improvement, not a defect -- but it is a real coupling to decide deliberately, not a free move. [Design-foundation reviewer. I judge ideas on real design value -- visual hierarchy, accessibility, theme consistency, layout reuse, design-review rigour -- and on eligibility within the four-surface discipline: an idea that authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a static background/theme is INELIGIBLE (surface-blending or F016 execution). I reward governance/linting/evidence seams that make the design layer CHECKABLE and discount cosmetic ideas with no durable seam.

I read the design corpus on disk before scoring rather than trusting the supplied history: design/tokens/ (two files: tower-retail, executive-dark), design/grids/ (16x9-grid.yaml, mobile-grid.yaml), themes/ (two theme JSONs + executive-dark.theme-spec.md), reports/blueprints/ (four filled blueprints), and the ten live design rules in src/seshat/rules/ (design_contrast CT1, design_ramp_deltae, design_categorical_distinctness, design_grid_closure DL5, design_review_evidence DL4, design_visual_selfcheck DL6, design_theme_fidelity DL3, design_theme, design_background, design_routes).

Three verified facts drove my design verdicts. (1) BOTH token files declare accessibility.min_text_contrast_ratio, and NEITHER declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae -- so c26's premise ("a dark dashboard can ship entirely un-contrast-checked") is false, and its proposed ERROR is structurally unfireable. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] Outside my design standpoint; deferring to the onboarding/docs lens on value rather than promoting it myself. What I can endorse from my angle is the honesty discipline: routing rather than restating, and pointing at each stage doc's OWN self-label instead of asserting a maturity claim -- the same declared-not-derived rule I require of design floors and of c23. The defect is verified by reading the file (a Claude Code usage-stats template behind the one obviously-named door) and the fix preserves the existing material under another name so ShareOnboardingGuide keeps working.

**Panel dissent:** Three reviewers ADOPT with strong scores (v7-8/f8-9); the design reviewer holds at CONSIDER (v6/f8), same score range, deferring on the onboarding-doc rewrite as outside their design-specific lane.

**First step:** Move the existing usage-stats content to a differently-named file, then write the four-part routing page pointing at each stage doc's own self-label.

At most one of the three should proceed; pick by site, not by pitch.

I judge ideas on real design value -- visual hierarchy, accessibility, theme consistency, layout reuse, design-review rigour -- and on eligibility within the four-surface discipline: an idea that authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a static background/theme is INELIGIBLE (surface-blending or F016 execution). I reward governance/linting/evidence seams that make the design layer CHECKABLE and discount cosmetic ideas with no durable seam.

I read the design corpus on disk before scoring rather than trusting the supplied history: design/tokens/ (two files: tower-retail, executive-dark), design/grids/ (16x9-grid.yaml, mobile-grid.yaml), themes/ (two theme JSONs + executive-dark.theme-spec.md), reports/blueprints/ (four filled blueprints), and the ten live design rules in src/seshat/rules/ (design_contrast CT1, design_ramp_deltae, design_categorical_distinctness, design_grid_closure DL5, design_review_evidence DL4, design_visual_selfcheck DL6, design_theme_fidelity DL3, design_theme, design_background, design_routes).

Three verified facts drove my design verdicts. (1) BOTH token files declare accessibility.min_text_contrast_ratio, and NEITHER declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae -- so c26's premise ("a dark dashboard can ship entirely un-contrast-checked") is false, and its proposed ERROR is structurally unfireable. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] NOT challenged by the skeptic (its array covers 45 of 46 candidates and omits this one), so no objection was tested against it -- I mark it survived by absence of challenge, not by adversarial clearance, and that is a caveat on the score rather than a strength. Partly in my lane as a presentation/routing-surface concern: rendering a DECLARED categorical field at routing time, owner-authored and never derived from file counts or directory shape, is the correct no-fabricated-confidence posture, and the same declared-not-derived discipline I require of design floors. The verified structural asymmetry (only bi-python carries a status line in SKILL.md; retail-kpi's 'initial seed' caveat sits in a README; bi-analyst is structurally lighter yet treated as a full peer) is real. Because it went unchallenged, treat the scores as provisional until someone tests the duplication objection against knowledge-map.md.

**Panel dissent:** The principle and duplication auditors both ADOPT (v6-7/f8); the value/feasibility realist and design reviewer hold at CONSIDER (v6/f8) on the same scores -- a verdict-threshold disagreement rather than a facts dispute.

**First step:** Add the declared frontmatter field to all six SKILL.md files with the owner filling each value, then render the column in knowledge-map.md's six-route table.

(3) A candidate that NAMES the shipped feature it extends and says which it is (c33 naming DL4, c29 quoting DL6's own deferral, c43 and c41 explicitly reinforcing FHC) is a genuine extension and I did not penalize it -- its weaknesses land in value/feasibility instead. But feasibility fails on the join: the key is a dimension NAME matched between two independently authored prose surfaces with no controlled vocabulary, so 'product' versus 'dim_product' silently misses, and the safeguard (list unmatched on both sides) means the honest output is two unmatched lists. I reward governance/linting/evidence seams that make the design layer CHECKABLE and discount cosmetic ideas with no durable seam.

I read the design corpus on disk before scoring rather than trusting the supplied history: design/tokens/ (two files: tower-retail, executive-dark), design/grids/ (16x9-grid.yaml, mobile-grid.yaml), themes/ (two theme JSONs + executive-dark.theme-spec.md), reports/blueprints/ (four filled blueprints), and the ten live design rules in src/seshat/rules/ (design_contrast CT1, design_ramp_deltae, design_categorical_distinctness, design_grid_closure DL5, design_review_evidence DL4, design_visual_selfcheck DL6, design_theme_fidelity DL3, design_theme, design_background, design_routes).

Three verified facts drove my design verdicts. (1) BOTH token files declare accessibility.min_text_contrast_ratio, and NEITHER declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae -- so c26's premise ("a dark dashboard can ship entirely un-contrast-checked") is false, and its proposed ERROR is structurally unfireable. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] Mostly outside my standpoint; deferring on the analytics. The join-reliability objection is the decisive one and I share it: matching a caveat's affected dimension to a question's cites.dimensions across two independently authored prose surfaces with no controlled vocabulary silently misses 'product' against 'dim_product', so the honest output is two unmatched lists. Note also that c40 depends on this for its content, so parking this parks that.

**Panel dissent:** Two reviewers CONSIDER (v5/f4); the value/feasibility realist and design reviewer drop to PARK, discounting feasibility because the caveat-to-decision map needs a filled qualification table the project doesn't yet have.

**First step:** Establish the controlled dimension vocabulary first -- without a shared key the join is a string match across free text and its honest output is two unmatched lists.

I read the design corpus on disk before scoring rather than trusting the supplied history: design/tokens/ (two files: tower-retail, executive-dark), design/grids/ (16x9-grid.yaml, mobile-grid.yaml), themes/ (two theme JSONs + executive-dark.theme-spec.md), reports/blueprints/ (four filled blueprints), and the ten live design rules in src/seshat/rules/ (design_contrast CT1, design_ramp_deltae, design_categorical_distinctness, design_grid_closure DL5, design_review_evidence DL4, design_visual_selfcheck DL6, design_theme_fidelity DL3, design_theme, design_background, design_routes).

Three verified facts drove my design verdicts. (1) BOTH token files declare accessibility.min_text_contrast_ratio, and NEITHER declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae -- so c26's premise ("a dark dashboard can ship entirely un-contrast-checked") is false, and its proposed ERROR is structurally unfireable. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] Presentation-adjacent, so I have a partial view: honest about computing nothing, which matters. But value collapses to re-rendering committed prose into a second document, and it is one of three overlapping caveat surfaces (with c18, c39) competing for the same reader-trust slot. On the live corpus it mostly restates one filled brief. Park until the reader-trust family is consolidated to one surface.

**Panel dissent:** Unanimous PARK with consistent scores; no material disagreement.

**First step:** None.

(1) BOTH token files declare accessibility.min_text_contrast_ratio, and NEITHER declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae -- so c26's premise ("a dark dashboard can ship entirely un-contrast-checked") is false, and its proposed ERROR is structurally unfireable. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] Outside my standpoint; deferring. Materially-new relative to the SUPPLIED history but the skeptic found CB1 live on disk already requiring a primary date field on comparison contracts -- the same partial-ship-status defect I confirmed for the design family. Surviving residue is only the table-level declaration plus the non-reconciliation note for non-comparison metrics, which is thin.

**Panel dissent:** The principle auditor alone rules REJECT (v3/f6) on grounds that the shipped CB1/HR5 rules already force the load-bearing fields this candidate wants to declare; the other three score it PARK, seeing residual value in the reconciliation-note half of the idea.

**First step:** None.

I reward the two candidates whose acceptance oracle ALREADY SHIPS (c22's rule-fix bijection test, c19's check_measure_drift inverse verifier) -- that is the difference between feasible-in-principle and feasible-this-week. Verified independently: rule_fix_table.py has only a script and its test as consumers; dax_gen.py:262 refuses anything but base|ratio while the variance template ships; zero freshness hits in templates/handoff/; ADR 0002 defines RC1/RC5/RC11/RC14 with rationale (killing c3's premise); no SKILL.md carries a frontmatter status line (strengthening c23 beyond its own claim). Note the skeptic array omits c23 entirely, so I scored it on my own read.] The value judgment is easy: grain fan-out is the defect class that most directly corrupts a business number, an inflated revenue total passes every current gate, and leaving 'which grain is correct' to a human is the right posture. The feasibility judgment is equally clear and it is the reason this parks. The load-bearing leg is inferring grain from AUTHORED SQL, which is a parser; the candidate's own refusal-first posture means the common case escalates, and a rule that fires rarely and escalates often on the highest-stakes defect trains operators to dismiss it. The two claims comparable WITHOUT parsing (source-map grain versus contract grain) are the buildable slice and are partly covered by the mapping gate already. Park until someone scopes that narrow slice honestly. [Design-foundation reviewer. I judge ideas on real design value -- visual hierarchy, accessibility, theme consistency, layout reuse, design-review rigour -- and on eligibility within the four-surface discipline: an idea that authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a static background/theme is INELIGIBLE (surface-blending or F016 execution). I reward governance/linting/evidence seams that make the design layer CHECKABLE and discount cosmetic ideas with no durable seam.

I read the design corpus on disk before scoring rather than trusting the supplied history: design/tokens/ (two files: tower-retail, executive-dark), design/grids/ (16x9-grid.yaml, mobile-grid.yaml), themes/ (two theme JSONs + executive-dark.theme-spec.md), reports/blueprints/ (four filled blueprints), and the ten live design rules in src/seshat/rules/ (design_contrast CT1, design_ramp_deltae, design_categorical_distinctness, design_grid_closure DL5, design_review_evidence DL4, design_visual_selfcheck DL6, design_theme_fidelity DL3, design_theme, design_background, design_routes).

Three verified facts drove my design verdicts. (1) BOTH token files declare accessibility.min_text_contrast_ratio, and NEITHER declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae -- so c26's premise ("a dark dashboard can ship entirely un-contrast-checked") is false, and its proposed ERROR is structurally unfireable. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] Outside my standpoint; deferring. Grain fan-out is the right defect to fear and human-rules-it is the right posture, but the SQL-grain-inference leg IS the idea and is the leg it cannot deliver -- a refusal-first parse escalates on the common case, training operators to dismiss the highest-stakes finding. This repo has a documented regex-over-structured-text burn.

**Panel dissent:** Unanimous PARK, though feasibility scores vary widely (f2 to f3) -- reviewers agree on the verdict but weight the medallion-hop mechanism's difficulty differently.

**First step:** None.

(3) A candidate that NAMES the shipped feature it extends and says which it is (c33 naming DL4, c29 quoting DL6's own deferral, c43 and c41 explicitly reinforcing FHC) is a genuine extension and I did not penalize it -- its weaknesses land in value/feasibility instead. So a Stage-1/2-keyed candidate has 3-4 real instances; a Stage-5/7 candidate that FOLDS ACROSS tables has nothing to fold; but a Stage-5/7 candidate that only authors a FIELD SHAPE is fine on one instance. I reward the two candidates whose acceptance oracle ALREADY SHIPS (c22's rule-fix bijection test, c19's check_measure_drift inverse verifier) -- that is the difference between feasible-in-principle and feasible-this-week. Note the skeptic array omits c23 entirely, so I scored it on my own read.] Worst feasibility-to-value ratio among the design candidates. The prerequisite is annotating fifteen hand-maintained HTML files with per-block token provenance -- substantial owner-authored work before a single check runs -- and coverage is then exactly as good as the annotation discipline of files that are already drifting, since undeclared blocks are skipped. The declared-correspondence-only posture is the right discipline and the drift is real. But the harm prevented is a stale hex in a developer-facing preview gallery: no business number is wrong, no gate is fooled, no reader is misled. I reward governance/linting/evidence seams that make the design layer CHECKABLE and discount cosmetic ideas with no durable seam.

I read the design corpus on disk before scoring rather than trusting the supplied history: design/tokens/ (two files: tower-retail, executive-dark), design/grids/ (16x9-grid.yaml, mobile-grid.yaml), themes/ (two theme JSONs + executive-dark.theme-spec.md), reports/blueprints/ (four filled blueprints), and the ten live design rules in src/seshat/rules/ (design_contrast CT1, design_ramp_deltae, design_categorical_distinctness, design_grid_closure DL5, design_review_evidence DL4, design_visual_selfcheck DL6, design_theme_fidelity DL3, design_theme, design_background, design_routes).

Three verified facts drove my design verdicts. (1) BOTH token files declare accessibility.min_text_contrast_ratio, and NEITHER declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae -- so c26's premise ("a dark dashboard can ship entirely un-contrast-checked") is false, and its proposed ERROR is structurally unfireable. (2) `grep -rln` for the dotted token pointers returns exactly ONE consumer, blueprint_preview.py -- no RULE resolves any of the five `*_ref` dotted paths or `grid_ref`/`tokens_ref`, a verified dead pointer graph (c27). (3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] MY LANE. The drift is real (design/claude-design-system/ exists alongside the token source) and the declared-correspondence-only posture matches DL3's discipline, which I credit. But the cost/benefit fails my test twice: it requires first annotating 15 hand-maintained HTML files with token-provenance headers, and coverage is then only as good as the annotation discipline of files that are ALREADY drifting -- undeclared blocks are skipped. And the thing protected is a developer-facing preview gallery: a stale hex there misleads a designer but reaches no business reader and no gate, so it ranks below c27/c29 which protect the pointer graph and the layout vocabulary the blueprints actually depend on.

**Panel dissent:** Unanimous PARK; no material dissent.

**First step:** None.

And it depends on c34 for its content, which I parked. Revisit only after a caveat-content surface ships and after c29 fixes the zone vocabulary it would reserve against.

(3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] Outside my standpoint; deferring. Feedback on imitation is a real learning gap and the guards (executes nothing, writes nothing into the learner's tree, cannot mark a stage pass, a clean diff proves imitation not readiness) are well drawn. But the generic-only constraint forces the shape/content line to be drawn by per-field judgment: draw it wrong and the kit tells a learner their correct business rule is wrong; draw it safely and it degrades to file-presence checking, which teaches nothing.

**Panel dissent:** Unanimous PARK; no material dissent.

**First step:** None.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. I read the design corpus on disk before scoring rather than trusting the supplied history: design/tokens/ (two files: tower-retail, executive-dark), design/grids/ (16x9-grid.yaml, mobile-grid.yaml), themes/ (two theme JSONs + executive-dark.theme-spec.md), reports/blueprints/ (four filled blueprints), and the ten live design rules in src/seshat/rules/ (design_contrast CT1, design_ramp_deltae, design_categorical_distinctness, design_grid_closure DL5, design_review_evidence DL4, design_visual_selfcheck DL6, design_theme_fidelity DL3, design_theme, design_background, design_routes).

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. The remainder is speculative shape for a source class the kit has never seen. Another instance of the partial ship-status list producing a false novelty claim.

**Panel dissent:** The principle auditor alone calls this SHIPPED (v2/f5) while the other three independently verified it is NOT covered and REJECT it on low value instead -- the auditor's duplication claim did not survive the other panelists' checks.

(3) All four filled blueprints draw section names only from the 16x9 grid's `zones` set, and 16x9-grid.yaml is the ONLY file declaring `zones` (mobile declares `safe_zones` bands) -- so c29's canonical authority source is structurally determined, not an owner ruling, and DL6's docstring defers exactly this cross-file parity verbatim.

Confirmed the skeptic's transferable defect: the supplied ship-status list is PARTIAL relative to src/seshat/rules/. CB1, HR4, HR5, HR13 and the entire DL/CT design family are live on disk and absent from it, so for c26-c33 and c40 I set prior_status from disk, not from the supplied history. Any candidate whose novelty argument rested on "verified against the known-history list" (c40 explicitly; c33/c34/c37 lean on it) has an unsound novelty argument regardless of whether a duplicate exists.

Eligibility outcome: NO candidate in this set is ineligible on the four-surface test. Every design candidate reads committed YAML/markdown and emits findings; none authors a PBIP/PBIR, generates DAX, invents a metric, or bakes data into a background or theme. c40 is the closest call (it governs caveat PLACEMENT) but it declares a convention plus a static assertion and renders nothing, so it stays eligible and fails on mechanism instead.

For the ~33 candidates outside my expertise (knowledge-layer routing, KPI contract semantics, CLI composition, onboarding) I DEFER: mid-range scores, verdict driven by the skeptic's disposition, low confidence flagged in the rationale. I did not manufacture design-adjacent reasoning to promote survivors I happen to find convincing (c22, c41) -- the skeptic's survivor list is not my mandate to ADOPT.] Killed on a false premise the skeptic verified: RC1-RC16 are already defined with rationale in docs/decisions/0002-retail-cleaning-defaults.md, and the constitution ratifies them. A second RC catalog is exactly the duplicated-status-claim rot SC1/SC2 exist to police. Outside my standpoint but the kill is factual, not judgmental.

**Panel dissent:** Three reviewers rule this SHIPPED (already exists as ADR 0002's RC1-RC16 catalog) while the design-foundation reviewer, outside their domain, scored it REJECT on low value rather than confirming the duplication finding.

**First step:** None.

## Rescue notes (steelman of the not-adopted)

_A reframing that MIGHT make an idea eligible later, or the irreducible reason it stays out. A reason for a human to weigh next run -- never a verdict; the gate already ruled._

- **Bilingual Report Round Trip -- term dictionary to report vocabulary** -- rescue possible. Do not build the read-side route now. Instead, author one FILLED bilingual term-dictionary instance for retail_store_sales (the only table with a filled report-vocabulary.yaml today), scoped to the terms report-vocabulary.yaml already carries. That turns 'read the confirmed rows' from a permanently-empty set into a testable one-instance case, and the existing load-time refusal (no key may name a value/metric) can then be exercised for real instead of theoretically. Narrowed seam: A single owner-authored templates/retail-term-dictionary.md instance under mappings/retail_store_sales/, populated only for terms already in that table's report-vocabulary.yaml -- not a kit feature, an authored content seam.
- **Guardrail Explainer -- the footnote a caveat code is missing** -- rescue possible. Narrow to exactly the case the panel conceded is safe: render the counterfactual ONLY where narrative-brief.md already commits the alternate-choice number in prose (the 33.55% case), and emit [GAP] everywhere else with no attempt to compute. That removes the fabrication-adjacent temptation entirely since the mechanism becomes 'quote an already-written number,' not 'compute one.' Narrowed seam: A quote-only extractor keyed to brief passages that explicitly state a counterfactual value in committed prose -- never invoked where the brief is silent.
- **Contract Meaning Transfer -- from named KPI contract to a filled instance** -- no rescue. Verified true duplicate: kpi-contract-builder/SKILL.md plus kpi_contracts.py already ship exactly this deliverable (per-field provenance block + gap list + owner-decision split). No reframing changes what the shipped verb already does; the seam is occupied, not narrow.
- **Candidate Promotion Docket -- the review packet analyzer_v2 candidates never get** -- rescue possible. Decouple from c1 entirely. Even if c1 (the candidate promotion workflow) never ships, a standalone read-only docket that projects false_positive_risk/detectability/required_inputs from the four analyzer-rule-candidates.json files onto ONE page has independent value for anyone auditing the candidate backlog by hand today, without needing a promotion workflow to consume it. Narrowed seam: A static projection page with zero dependency on any promotion verb -- pure read-only rendering of already-committed JSON, valuable as a standalone audit aid.
- **Layer Handoff Trace -- follow one decision across the six knowledge layers** -- no rescue. No shared decision-id key exists across the artifacts it must walk (A-codes, Q-ids, gold_placement all use different numbering) -- this is a substrate absence, not a scoping problem, and no narrower slice changes that the forward-citation key nobody wrote.
- **Metric-Lineage Answer Card: from a business question to the columns behind it** -- rescue possible. Hold until a second table reaches Stage 5+ (metric contracts + design + handoff). At that point the metric-first, decision-first framing this candidate proposes would have genuinely new content to organize across tables, rather than restating one table's existing consumer-data-dictionary and assumptions ledger. Narrowed seam: Re-trigger condition: two or more tables with filled metrics/ + narrative-brief.md + handoff/, so the composition spans real cross-table content instead of reordering one table's existing artifacts.
- **Feed-Unlock Ledger: rank absent sources by countable KPI unlock** -- rescue possible. Strip all ranking/ordering language. Ship as a plain grouped listing: for each absent feed, the count of currently-blocked KPI contracts it would unlock, grouped alphabetically or by feed name -- never sorted by count, never phrased as priority. That satisfies never_fabricate_a_confidence_score because a count is measured, not scored, and removing the sort removes the categorical-ranking-in-disguise problem the auditor named. Narrowed seam: An unordered, un-sorted per-feed unlock-count listing with no ranking language anywhere in the output -- render order fixed by feed registration order or alphabetical, never by the count itself.
- **Unresolved-Ambiguity Register per project (A1-A11 applied, not just enumerated)** -- rescue possible. Do not seed a new register. Instead extend the existing per-contract ambiguities[] ledger with an explicit cross-contract scope flag (e.g. scope: table-wide) for the specific A-codes that are genuinely table-spanning (VAT basis, date axis), so the shared axis is visible as an annotation on the existing substrate rather than a new rolled-up surface that starts entirely red. Narrowed seam: A scope field added to the ambiguities[] entries HR5 already reads, not a new document -- makes the cross-contract axis discoverable without duplicating the ledger.
- **Date-Axis Declaration and reconciliation note** -- rescue possible. Drop the axis-declaration claim entirely since CB1 already owns it. Keep only the narrow residue CB1 does not reach: an explicit non-reconciliation note attached to NON-comparison metrics, stating plainly that no date-axis reconciliation is claimed for them. Fold this one field into c12's field-shape work rather than treating it as a standalone idea. Narrowed seam: A single boolean/note field on non-comparison metric contracts: 'date-axis reconciliation not applicable/not claimed' -- attached to c12, not shipped alone.
- **Cross-Star Metric Comparability Note** -- rescue possible. Correctly identified as YAGNI-blocked by the panel itself: re-propose the moment a second star with a metric-name overlap actually lands. No work should be banked now; the seam is well-specified and cheap to build later. Narrowed seam: Trigger condition: two committed gold stars sharing a metric name. Until then, no seam exists to build.
- **Semi-Additive Snapshot Contract Shape for the inventory class** -- rescue possible. The time_additivity field is already shipped and enforced by HR5 -- drop that claim entirely. The residual, unduplicated seam is the three fields HR5 does not touch: snapshot_grain, periodic-vs-accumulating, and last_observation_policy. Re-propose narrowly as an extension to HR5's existing vocabulary once the kit actually onboards a semi-additive/snapshot source class (inventory, balances) that needs those fields. Narrowed seam: Extend HR5's contract shape with snapshot_grain / periodic_vs_accumulating / last_observation_policy fields -- gated on an actual snapshot-type source arriving, not built speculatively.
- **Business-Question Entry Index across the six knowledge layers** -- no rescue. Structurally a third ungated routing surface duplicating knowledge-map.md and six INDEX.md files with no runtime consumer to keep it true -- the exact drift class A3's bijection and SC1/SC2 exist to prevent. c41's routing-page approach already covers the same reader need without adding an unenforced parallel map.
- **Volume-Regime Advisory: bind the bigdata layer to the source profile** -- rescue possible. Drop the sizing verdict (single-node-fine vs. partitioning-question-open) entirely -- that is the fabrication-adjacent part. Keep only a pure route: read the profile's row-count/volume evidence and name which bigdata checklist document to open next, stating no regime classification at all. This is a router, not a judge. Narrowed seam: A route-only function: volume evidence in -> checklist filename out, zero categorical regime label, honest [PENDING LIVE PROFILE] when evidence is absent.
- **Grain-Regression Guard across the medallion hops** -- rescue possible. Drop the SQL-inference leg entirely -- that is the part that cannot be delivered reliably per the repo's own regex-over-structured-text lesson. Keep only the buildable slice both value reviewers named: compare source-map declared grain against contract declared grain WITHOUT parsing any SQL, since both are already structured YAML fields the mapping gate can read directly. Narrowed seam: A static comparison of two already-structured fields (source-map grain vs. contract grain), reported as a human-decides blocker on mismatch -- no SQL parsing, no authored-code inference.
- **Theme-Pair Declaration Coverage Report (which design artifacts opted into which floor)** -- rescue possible. Invert the check. The design-foundation reviewer's own grep found the real gap: neither token file declares min_adjacent_delta_e or min_categorical_deltae, so design_ramp_deltae.py and design_categorical_distinctness.py silently skip on the entire live corpus. Re-propose as an ERROR/WARN when a colour-carrying tokens file declares SOME accessibility floors but omits these two specific ones -- reporting UN-OPTED-IN floors, not a census of files with zero floors (which cannot fire). Narrowed seam: A rule that fires when a tokens file declares min_text_contrast_ratio but omits min_adjacent_delta_e/min_categorical_deltae -- the condition that is actually true on disk today, unlike the original's unfireable premise.
- **Spacing and Type-Scale Rhythm Closure Check** -- rescue possible. Already partially rescued by the panel itself (CONSIDER verdict, arithmetic leg kept). Drop the type-scale monotonic-ordering leg entirely since it invents a hierarchy the tokens file never declares. Ship only the base_unit_px multiple-of-base arithmetic check, scoped honestly as checking the one file that already satisfies it -- valuable as a regression guard for future spacing additions, not as a defect-finder today. Narrowed seam: base_unit_px multiple-of arithmetic only, framed as a regression guard against future drift rather than a claim of catching a current defect.
- **Mobile Reflow-Fate Contract (a blueprint may not claim a desktop_only band survives)** -- rescue possible. Sequence behind c29 (which the design reviewer says resolves the same band-vocabulary question). Once an owner rules a structured retained_bands/deviations shape for mobile_notes, and once the 'reason' field is replaced with an enum of specific named justifications rather than free text, the gate stops being a caller-supplied precondition and becomes a real structural check. Narrowed seam: After c29 lands: mobile_notes.retained_bands as a structured list validated against mobile-grid.yaml's fate vocabulary, with deviations requiring a closed-vocabulary reason code, not free text.
- **Peer-Theme Parity Contract (a second theme must be a structural peer of the first)** -- rescue possible. Skip the rule/contract-doc machinery. Since the corpus is only two files and DL3 already covers value fidelity, the residual key-set/data_colors-cardinality check is cheap enough to fold as one additional assertion INTO DL3 rather than standing up a separate opt-in rule with its own meta.peer_of field. Narrowed seam: One additional structural-parity assertion added to DL3's existing token-to-theme reconciliation, not a new rule.
- **Preview-Gallery Token Drift Detector (the 15 HTML previews vs the YAML tokens)** -- rescue possible. Re-scope to new previews only: require token-provenance annotation as a condition of adding any NEW preview file going forward (enforced at PR time for new files), rather than retrofitting all 15 existing ones. This avoids the large annotation debt while still preventing new drift. Narrowed seam: A forward-only annotation requirement on newly-added preview HTML files, leaving the 15 existing ones unannotated and unchecked.
- **Open-Decision Reader Brief -- what the A-code actually asks you** -- rescue possible. Drop the 'state both candidate directions categorically' framing that edges toward pre-ruling. Keep only a register-translation layer that restates the A-code's technical description in plain owner language WITHOUT characterizing what either answer would mean numerically -- purely a vocabulary translation, not a framed choice. Narrowed seam: A translation-only rendering of the A-code's existing technical text into plain language, with zero characterization of consequences of either answer.
- **Cross-Table Question Router -- can I answer this anywhere?** -- rescue possible. Re-propose once at least two or three tables reach Stage 5+ with filled answerability-summary.md instances, so the cross-table fold has real content to fold and can be tested against actual divergence between tables. Narrowed seam: Trigger condition: 2+ filled templates/handoff/answerability-summary.md instances across tables -- currently zero exist anywhere in mappings/.
- **Definition-Change Notice for the Returning Reader** -- rescue possible. Fix the small missing prerequisite first: have the approval-decision record capture the pinned revision set AT THE MOMENT of publish-approval (not just a dated approval timestamp). Once that snapshot exists as a committed artifact, the diff-against-baseline mechanism this candidate proposes becomes buildable without inventing an unstated git-history diff. Narrowed seam: Add a revision-set snapshot field to the approval-decision record at publish-approval time; the notice itself is a small follow-on once that field exists.
- **Caveat and Provenance Placement Convention** -- rescue possible. Sequence behind whichever caveat-content candidate actually ships (not c34, which was parked) and behind c29's zone-vocabulary fix. Once both land, re-scope the check to verify placement against the RESOLVED zone vocabulary rather than an author-supplied claim -- turning the precondition-supplied-by-caller pattern into a structural check against a fixed enum. Narrowed seam: Placement check validated against c29's resolved zone vocabulary plus whatever caveat-content surface ships, rather than a free-text author claim.
- **Wrong-surface router: when someone asks for a capability by the wrong name** -- rescue possible. Replace the unbounded hand-maintained alias table with a single durable sentence in the capabilities surface (the one c41's routing page already carries) stating why the no-CLI-verb decision exists. This delivers the real need -- discoverability of the ratified absence -- without an identity-fragile, unbounded maintenance burden. Narrowed seam: One static explanatory sentence in the shipped capabilities inventory / c41's routing page, keyed to the ratified decision id, not to guessed label strings.
- **Cold-start dry run: walk the worked example end to end and diff against the committed result** -- rescue possible. Restrict to the narrowest field class where shape-vs-content genuinely has no ambiguity: file PRESENCE and required-key presence only (does the learner's output have the same top-level keys/sections as the worked example), never comparing values. This is honestly just file-presence checking, but framed as exactly that rather than pretending to test understanding -- useful as a completeness checklist, not a correctness check. Narrowed seam: A required-keys/sections presence diff only, explicitly labeled as a completeness check, not a correctness or understanding check.
- **One reader-facing identity per feature across the three numbering axes** -- rescue possible. Skip the new identity-record surface entirely. Directly fix the verified defect: correct the F016 status divergence in AGENTS.md, COMPASS.md, and readiness-model.md to reflect that slices 1-5 shipped. This is an edit to existing docs, not a new idea or a new hand-maintained source. Narrowed seam: A one-time documentation correction to the three named files' F016 status line -- not a standing feature.
- **Name the human seam in the reader's own words at every approval gate** -- rescue possible. Reduce scope drastically: instead of a new four-paragraph owner-authored surface, add ONE optional free-text 'in plain terms' field to the existing approval-request schema that J1/approval-console already render, letting whoever drafts the approval-request add the plain-language framing inline rather than standing up a parallel surface. Narrowed seam: One optional field on the existing approval-request schema, rendered by the existing J1/approval-console surface -- no new slot, no new document.
- **Knowledge-Route Answer Evals: does the layer actually answer its own routing table?** -- no rescue. never_fabricate_a_confidence_score is a hard-stop, not a budget: any per-route pass/fail verdict from an LLM judge carries the same unearned categorical authority whether aggregated or not. No narrowing of scope removes the judge-issuing-a-verdict mechanism, only removing the judge entirely would -- at which point it is no longer this idea.
- **Trust Strip -- the four things a reader should check before deciding** -- no rescue. The candidate's entire content is duplicated across three other candidates (c34, c35, c36) by its own admission; only a presentation convention (fixed slot order) remains, which is not an idea but a constraint to attach to whichever content candidate ships -- there is no standalone seam left to narrow to.
